B.2
HEFT

In this work, we adopt the conventions of Ref.
[
71
]
for the LO and NLO HEFT operator bases. For NNLO, we are aware of the complete basis constructed in Ref.
[
72
]
, which extends the NLO basis of Ref.
[
73
]
. However, we employ a slightly modified NNLO basis, closer in structure to that of Ref.
[
71
]
, in which the operators are written in a form more directly comparable to their SMEFT counterparts. This choice significantly simplifies the comparison between the two EFT frameworks. Where necessary, we provide the correspondence between operators in our basis and those in Ref.
[
72
]
. This approach is justified since our analysis focuses on a single process, sensitive only to a subset of the NNLO HEFT operators.

B.2.1
HEFT at NLO

We use the following set of NLO HEFT operators in the basis of Ref.
[
71
]
,

\mathcal{L}_{\text{NLO}}^{\text{SMEFT}}=\mathcal{P}(h)_{DH}+\mathcal{P}(h)_{GH}+\mathcal{P}(h)_{\tilde{G}H}\,,

(63)

with,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(h)_{DH}

\displaystyle=\left(\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(h)_{DH}\,\partial^{\mu}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(h)_{DH}\right)^{2},\quad\mathcal{F}^{(\prime)}_{DH}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}C_{DH}^{(\prime)(n)}\left(\frac{h}{v}\right)^{n},

(64)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(h)_{GH}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}g_{s}^{2}G_{\mu\nu}^{A}G^{A\mu\nu}\mathcal{F}_{GH},\quad\mathcal{F}_{GH}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}C_{GH}^{(n)}\left(\frac{h}{v}\right)^{n},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(h)_{\tilde{G}H}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}g_{s}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\mu\nu}^{A}G^{A\mu\nu}\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{G}H},\quad\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{G}H}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}C_{\tilde{G}H}^{(n)}\left(\frac{h}{v}\right)^{n}.

The operator
\mathcal{P}(h)_{DH}
corresponds to
\mathcal{O}_{15}^{UhD^{4}}
in Ref.
[
73
]
, while operators

\mathcal{P}(h)_{GH}
and
\mathcal{P}(h)_{\tilde{G}H}
match

\mathcal{O}_{3}^{X^{2}Uh}
and
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{X^{2}Uh}
.

B.2.2
HEFT at NNLO

We use the following set of NNLO HEFT operators in our own basis, similar to NLO basis of Ref.
[
71
]
,

\mathcal{L}_{\text{NNLO}}^{\text{HEFT}}=\mathcal{P}(h)_{GHD}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}(h)_{GHD}^{(2)}+\mathcal{P}(h)_{\tilde{G}HD}\,.

(65)

with,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(h)_{GHD}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}g_{s}^{2}(\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\alpha}h)G_{\mu\nu}^{A}G^{A\mu\nu}\mathcal{F}_{GHD},\quad\mathcal{F}_{GHD}^{(1)}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}C^{(n)}_{GHD(1)}\left(\frac{h}{v}\right)^{n},

(66)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(h)_{GHD}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}g_{s}^{2}(\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\nu}h)G_{\mu\alpha}^{A}G^{A\mu\nu}\mathcal{F}_{GHD},\quad\mathcal{F}_{GHD}^{(2)}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}C^{(n)}_{GHD(2)}\left(\frac{h}{v}\right)^{n},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(h)_{\tilde{G}HD}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}g_{s}^{2}(\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\alpha}h)\tilde{G}_{\mu\nu}^{A}G^{A\mu\nu}\mathcal{F}_{GHD},\quad\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{G}HD}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}C^{(n)}_{\tilde{G}HD}\left(\frac{h}{v}\right)^{n}.

Alternatively, one can use the NNLO basis of Ref.
[
72
]
, with the following translation between the two bases,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{G_{L}^{2}h^{2}D^{2}}+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(h)_{GHD}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}(h)_{\tilde{G}HD},

(67)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{G_{L}h^{2}G_{R}D^{2}}+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(h)_{GHD}^{(2)}.

B.2.3
HEFT at N
3
LO

Additionally, we included the following N
3
LO operator used for generating a non-zero
c_{gghhh}^{++,(2)}
on-shell contribution,

\mathcal{P}(h)_{GHD^{4}}^{(1)}=-\frac{1}{4}g_{s}^{2}(\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}h\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}h)G_{\mu\alpha}^{A}G^{A\nu\beta}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}_{GHD^{4}},\quad\mathcal{F}_{GHD^{4}}^{(1)}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}C_{GHD^{4}(1)}^{(n)}\left(\frac{h}{v}\right)^{n}.

(68)

Appendix C
Matching subtleties

In this appendix, we show in detail two phenomenons that are crucial to ensure a successful matching between bootstrapped and EFT on-shell scattering amplitudes. First, we show how the non-factorizable piece of the on-shell amplitude absorbs some contributions that, in the EFT, are generated by factorizable diagrams. Second, we show how the first phenomenon and non-trivial cancellations allow the on-shell amplitude to correctly account for momentum-dependent lower-point vertices in the EFT.

C.1
Absorption into non-factorizable pieces

The results of the four-point matching are enough to explain the importance of matching complete amplitudes. We take the
++00

gg\to hh
amplitude and consider the SMEFT matching results in Eq. (
123
), we will focus on the
C_{\varphi G}^{2}
contribution. This contribution arises via the gluon-field kinetic normalization induced by
Q_{\varphi G}
, which then causes the shift
C_{\varphi G}\to C_{\varphi G}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{G_{F}}\frac{C_{\varphi G}}{\Lambda^{2}}\right)
. This correlation between the
C_{\varphi G}^{2}
and
C_{\varphi G}
contributions can be observed in the SMEFT matching result for
c_{ggh}
, Eq. (
118
), but not in the one for
c_{gghh}^{++,(00)}
, Eq. (
123
). This indicates that the latter receives contributions from diagrams with virtual particles. Here, we will show how the latter contributions are absorbed by the non-factorizable piece. This phenomenon is commonplace when matching the on-shell amplitudes.

The pole structure of the
gg\to hh
amplitude tells us that the
++00
helicity configuration has only a pole along the s-channel and hence our on-shell amplitude is composed of,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{++00}^{ONS}=

\displaystyle i\,c_{gghh}^{++}[1|2]^{2}+c_{ggh}\,c_{3h}\frac{[1|2]^{2}}{s-m_{h}^{2}}

(69)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\,c_{gghh}^{++}[1|2]^{2}+\left(-6\sqrt{2}\,\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{G_{F}}\,\frac{C_{\varphi G}^{2}}{\Lambda^{4}}\right)\frac{[1|2]^{2}}{s-m_{h}^{2}}\,,

where we have used Eq. (
116
), (
118
), and kept the term proportional to
C_{\varphi G}^{2}
in their product.
The spinor structure yields
[1|2]^{2}=-s
.
On the other hand, we have computed the same amplitude in the SMEFT up to order
1/\Lambda^{4}
. Focusing on the piece proportional to
C_{\varphi G}^{2}
we get,

\mathcal{M}_{++00}^{SMEFT}=6\,\frac{\sqrt{2}}{G_{F}^{2}}\,\frac{C_{\varphi G}^{2}}{\Lambda^{4}}\frac{s^{2}}{s-m_{h}^{2}}+...,

(70)

where the
...
stands for all the other SMEFT contributions.
The result of the matching procedure is easily seen from a simple manipulation of
\mathcal{M}_{++00}^{SMEFT}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{++00}^{SMEFT}=

\displaystyle 6\,\frac{\sqrt{2}}{G_{F}^{2}}\,\frac{C_{\varphi G}^{2}}{\Lambda^{4}}\frac{s^{2}}{s-m_{h}^{2}}

(71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6\,\frac{\sqrt{2}}{G_{F}^{2}}\,\frac{C_{\varphi G}^{2}}{\Lambda^{4}}\frac{s\,(s-m_{h}^{2}+m_{h}^{2})}{s-m_{h}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6\,\frac{\sqrt{2}}{G_{F}^{2}}\,\frac{C_{\varphi G}^{2}}{\Lambda^{4}}s+6\frac{\sqrt{2}}{G_{F}^{2}}\,\frac{C_{\varphi G}^{2}}{\Lambda^{4}}m_{h}^{2}\frac{s}{s-m_{h}^{2}}.

The second term in the last line above matches exactly the last term in the last line in (
69
), while the first term in the last line of (
71
) has the right Lorentz dependence to be absorbed by the first term in the expansion of
c_{gghh}^{++}
, i.e.
c_{gghh}^{++,(00)}
. Hence, we get,

-i\,c_{gghh}^{++,(00)}=6\,\frac{\sqrt{2}}{G_{F}^{2}}\,\frac{C_{\varphi G}^{2}}{\Lambda^{4}},

(72)

which agrees with Eq. (
123
).

C.2
Cancellation of momentum dependence from three- and four-Higgs vertices

In this appendix, we show that the momentum-dependence in the three- and four-Higgs vertices induced by operators such as
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi D}
and
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi\Box}
does not represent an obstacle to match the EFT amplitudes with the bootstrapped on-shell ones. To illustrate this, we perform a fully analytical
matching between SMEFT and the bootstrapped on-shell three-, four-, and five-Higgs amplitudes considering only the SM,
C_{\varphi}
and
C_{\varphi D}
contributions
10
10

10

We notice that
Q_{\varphi\Box}
generates the same amplitudes than
Q_{\varphi D}
up to a constant overall factor, hence this proof would be equally valid for it and we omit it for simplicity.
.
As can be seen in App.
E
, the dim-6 operators only generate contributions to the constant piece in the on-shell coefficient
c_{4h}
,
c_{4h}^{(00)}
. Thus, the factorizable piece of the on-shell five-point amplitude will depend on the momenta only via the propagators. The SMEFT five-Higgs amplitude does not present this feature a priori due to the momentum-dependence introduced by
Q_{\varphi D}
.

Before showing how the discrepancy is solved, we review some useful kinematic relations. For
i,\,j,\,k,\,\ell,\,m\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}
, all different, momentum conservation dictates that,

s_{ij}=s_{k\ell}+s_{km}+s_{\ell m}-3\,m_{h}^{2}\,.

(73)

If we define the inverse propagator as,
\Delta_{ij}=s_{ij}-m_{h}^{2}
, it can be shown that,

\sum_{k\neq i}\Delta_{ik}=2m_{h}^{2},\quad\forall\,i\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}\,.

(74)

Finally, all the possible inverse propagators are related by the equation,

\sum_{i=1}^{4}\sum_{j>i}^{5}\Delta_{ij}=5\,m_{h}^{2}\,.

(75)

For simplicity and without loss of generality, we focus on terms of order
1/\Lambda^{4}
that are quadratic on
C_{\varphi D}
and absorb
\Lambda^{2}
inside the WC. We start by looking at the diagrams,

\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}(5h)=\hbox to82.6pt{\vbox to43.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.45686pt\lower-36.00798pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(79)

with
i,\,j,\,k\in\{3,4,5\}
. These diagrams in SMEFT contain the following terms,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}\left(5h\right)\supset

\displaystyle i\,\frac{2925}{16}\frac{C_{\varphi D}^{2}\lambda^{3}v^{7}}{\Delta_{12}\Delta_{34}}+i\,15C_{\varphi D}^{2}\,v^{5}\lambda^{2}\frac{\Delta_{12}+\Delta_{34}}{\Delta_{12}\,\Delta_{34}}

(80)

\displaystyle-i\,\frac{45}{4}C_{\varphi D}^{2}\,v^{5}\lambda^{2}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{j=3}^{5}\Delta_{ij}\,+\,\sum_{k=3}^{4}\Delta_{k5}}{\Delta_{12}\Delta_{34}}

\displaystyle-i\frac{3}{4}C_{\varphi D}^{2}\,v^{3}\lambda\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{j=3}^{5}\Delta_{ij}\,+\,\sum_{k=3}^{4}\Delta_{k5}}{\Delta_{12}}

\displaystyle-i\frac{3}{4}C_{\varphi D}^{2}\,v^{3}\lambda\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{j=3}^{5}\Delta_{ij}\,-\Delta_{34}+\,\sum_{k=3}^{4}\Delta_{k5}}{\Delta_{34}},

which can be simplified to,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}\left(5h\right)\supset

\displaystyle i\,\frac{9}{4}C_{\varphi D}^{2}v^{3}\lambda+i\frac{45}{2}C_{\varphi D}^{2}v^{5}\lambda^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{12}}+\frac{1}{\Delta_{34}}\right)

(81)

\displaystyle+i\frac{2025}{16}\frac{C_{\varphi D}^{2}\lambda^{3}v^{7}}{\Delta_{12}\Delta_{34}}+i\frac{3}{4}C_{\varphi D}^{2}v^{3}\lambda\left(\frac{\Delta_{34}}{\Delta_{12}}+\frac{\Delta_{12}}{\Delta_{34}}\right),

by using Eq. (
75
). The first three terms pose no problem for the matching to the on-shell amplitudes. However, the last term cannot be reproduced by them due to being a rational function of the propagators. In fact, the first dangerous term,
\propto\frac{\Delta_{34}}{\Delta_{12}}
, cancels out within this same class of diagrams once one considers the terms proportional to
\frac{1}{\Delta_{12}\Delta_{35}}
and
\frac{1}{\Delta_{12}\Delta_{45}}
, which in turn generate other dangerous pieces. In total, the relevant contributions from these diagrams are,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}\left(5h\right)\supset

\displaystyle i\,\frac{15}{2}C_{\varphi D}^{2}v^{3}\lambda+i\frac{45}{2}C_{\varphi D}^{2}v^{5}\lambda^{2}\left(\frac{91}{30\,\Delta_{12}}+\frac{1}{\Delta_{34}}+\frac{1}{\Delta_{35}}+\frac{1}{\Delta_{45}}\right)

(82)

\displaystyle+i\frac{2025}{16}\frac{C_{\varphi D}^{2}\lambda^{3}v^{7}}{\Delta_{12}}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{34}}+\frac{1}{\Delta_{35}}+\frac{1}{\Delta_{45}}\right)

\displaystyle+i\frac{3}{4}C_{\varphi D}^{2}v^{3}\lambda\left(\frac{\Delta_{12}}{\Delta_{34}}+\frac{\Delta_{12}}{\Delta_{35}}+\frac{\Delta_{12}}{\Delta_{45}}\right),

where we collected at the end the terms that cannot be reproduced within the on-shell approach.

These dangerous terms cancel out within the SMEFT amplitude when considering all diagrams, hence allowing for a clean match to the bootstrapped on-shell amplitude. Consider the class of diagrams,

\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}\left(5h\right)=\hbox to100.34pt{\vbox to57.79pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.45686pt\lower-50.23436pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

(83)

After several simplifications, one can see that this class of diagrams generates terms like,

\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}\left(5h\right)\supset\,-i\frac{3}{4}C_{\varphi D}^{2}v^{3}\lambda\left(\frac{\Delta_{12}}{\Delta_{34}}+\frac{\Delta_{12}}{\Delta_{35}}+\frac{\Delta_{12}}{\Delta_{45}}\right),

(84)

which exactly cancels the contribution from
\mathcal{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}\left(5h\right)
. This cancellation is not limited to an specific order in WCs. In addition,
\mathcal{M}_{5}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}\left(5h\right)
generates further dangerous terms that are cancelled against the following amplitude,

\mathcal{M}_{6}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}(5h)=\hbox to67.86pt{\vbox to72.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.45686pt\lower-64.46074pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{}{}{}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

Finally, notice that these three classes of amplitudes are of the same topology and we have differentiated them only for the sake of clarity.

Appendix D
On-shell amplitudes

In this section, we collect expression for all on-shell three-, four- and five-point on-shell amplitudes derived and used in this work.

D.1
Three-point

D.1.1
gg\to h

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,+};h\right)

\displaystyle=i\,\delta^{ab}\left[1|2\right]^{2}c_{ggh}\,.

(86)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,-};h\right)

\displaystyle=0\,.

(87)

D.1.2
hh\to h

\mathcal{M}\left(h;h;h\right)=i\,c_{3h}\,.

(88)

D.2
Four-point

D.2.1
gg\to hh

Non-factorizable contributions

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,+};h_{3};h_{4}\right)_{\text{NF}}=i\,\delta^{ab}\left[1|2\right]^{2}c_{gghh}^{++},\quad c_{gghh}^{++}=\frac{1}{\overline{\Lambda}^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\frac{{c_{gghh}^{++,(ij)}}}{\overline{\Lambda}^{2\left(i+j\right)}}s_{12}^{i}\ s_{13}^{j}\,.

(89)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,-};h_{3};h_{4}\right)_{\text{NF}}=\,i\,\delta^{ab}\left[1|\mathbf{3}-\mathbf{4}|2\rangle\right.^{2}c_{gghh}^{+-},\quad c_{gghh}^{+-}=\frac{1}{\overline{\Lambda}^{4}}\sum_{i,j}\frac{{c_{gghh}^{+-,(ij)}}}{\overline{\Lambda}^{2\left(i+j\right)}}s_{12}^{i}\ s_{13}^{j}\,.

(90)

Factorizable contributions

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,+};h_{3};h_{4}\right)_{s\text{--ch.}}=-\delta^{ab}\frac{c_{3h}\,c_{ggh}}{s_{12}-m_{h}^{2}}[1|2]^{2}\,.

(91)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,-};h_{3};h_{4}\right)_{t+u\text{--ch.}}=-\delta^{ab}\frac{|c_{ggh}|^{2}}{4}\left[1|\mathbf{3}-\mathbf{4}|2\rangle\right.^{2}\left(\frac{1}{s_{13}}+\frac{1}{s_{23}}\right)\,.

(92)

D.2.2
hh\to hh

Non-factorizable contribution

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(h;h;h;h\right)_{\text{NF}}=i\,c_{4h},\quad c_{4h}=\sum_{i,j}\frac{c_{4h}^{(ij)}}{\overline{\Lambda}^{2\left(i+j\right)}}s_{12}^{i}\ s_{13}^{j}\,.

(93)

Factorizable contributions

\mathcal{M}\left(h;h;h;h\right)_{s+t+u\text{--ch.}}=c_{3h}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{s_{12}-m_{h}^{2}}+\frac{1}{s_{13}-m_{h}^{2}}+\frac{1}{s_{23}-m_{h}^{2}}\right)\,.

(94)

D.3
Five-point

D.3.1
gg\to hhh

Non-factorizable contributions

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,+};h_{3};h_{4};h_{5}\right)_{\text{NF}}

\displaystyle=i\,\delta^{ab}\,c_{gghhh}^{++,\,(1)}[1|2]^{2}

\displaystyle+i\,\delta^{ab}\,c_{gghhh}^{++,\,(2)}\left([1|\mathbf{3}\mathbf{4}|2][1|\mathbf{4}\mathbf{3}|2]+[1|\mathbf{3}\mathbf{5}|2][1|\mathbf{5}\mathbf{3}|2]+[1|\mathbf{4}\mathbf{5}|2][1|\mathbf{5}\mathbf{4}|2]\right),

\displaystyle c_{gghhh}^{++,\,(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overline{\Lambda}^{3}}\sum_{i,j,k,l,m=0}\frac{c_{gghhh}^{++,\,(1)(ijklm)}}{\overline{\Lambda}^{2\left(i+j+k+l+m\right)}}s_{12}^{i}\ s_{13}^{j}\ s_{14}^{k}\ s_{23}^{l}\ s_{24}^{m}\,,

(95)

\displaystyle c_{gghhh}^{++,\,(2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overline{\Lambda}^{7}}\sum_{i,j,k,l,m=0}\frac{c_{gghhh}^{++,\,(2)(ijklm)}}{\overline{\Lambda}^{2\left(i+j+k+l+m\right)}}s_{12}^{i}\ s_{13}^{j}\ s_{14}^{k}\ s_{23}^{l}\ s_{24}^{m}\,.

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,-};h_{3};h_{4};h_{5}\right)_{\text{NF}}

\displaystyle=i\,\delta^{ab}\,c_{gghhh}^{+-}\left([1|\mathbf{3}{|2\rangle}{}^{2}+[1|\mathbf{4}{|2\rangle}{}^{2}+[1|\mathbf{5}{|2\rangle}{}^{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle c_{gghhh}^{+-}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overline{\Lambda}^{5}}\sum_{i,j,k,l,m=0}\frac{c_{gghhh}^{+-,\,(ijklm)}}{\overline{\Lambda}^{2\left(i+j+k+l+m\right)}}s_{12}^{i}\ s_{13}^{j}\ s_{14}^{k}\ s_{23}^{l}\ s_{24}^{m}\,.

(96)

Factorizable contributions

g_{1}
g_{2}
h_{5}
h_{4}
h_{3}
s_{12}

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,+};h_{3};h_{4};h_{5}\right)_{\text{F}1}=\delta^{ab}{[1|2]}^{2}\ \frac{c_{ggh}\ c_{4h}\left(s_{34},s_{35}\right)}{s_{12}-m_{h}^{2}}\,.

(97)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,-};h_{3};h_{4};h_{5}\right)_{\text{F}1}=0\,.

(98)

i\neq j\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{{{[i|}(i+\mathbf{3})(j+\mathbf{5})|j]}^{2}}{s_{i3}\,s_{j5}}+\frac{{{[i|}(i+\mathbf{5})(j+\mathbf{4})|j]}^{2}}{s_{i5}\,s_{j4}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{{{[i|}(i+\mathbf{5})(j+\mathbf{3})|j]}^{2}}{s_{i5}\,s_{j3}}\right).

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\left(g_{1}^{a,+};g_{2}^{b,-};h_{3};h_{4};h_{5}\right)_{\text{F}5}

\displaystyle=0\,.

\displaystyle=-\,i\,c_{3h}^{\,3}\times

(115)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\{1,2\}}\left(\frac{1}{s_{i5}-m_{h}^{2}}\,\frac{1}{s_{34}-m_{h}^{2}}+\frac{1}{s_{i4}-m_{h}^{2}}\,\frac{1}{s_{35}-m_{h}^{2}}+\frac{1}{s_{i3}-m_{h}^{2}}\,\frac{1}{s_{45}-m_{h}^{2}}\right)\,.

Appendix E
Complete matching results

In this appendix, we collect the complete results of matching the on-shell scattering amplitudes to dim-8 SMEFT or NNLO HEFT. For the matching to SMEFT, we identify the on-shell scale
\overline{\Lambda}
with the SMEFT cutoff
\Lambda
. The identification of
\overline{\Lambda}
with a HEFT parameter is discussed in the main text and here we present the HEFT results without equating
\overline{\Lambda}
to anything.

E.1
Three-point amplitudes

E.1.1
gg\to h

SMEFT:

\displaystyle-i\,c_{ggh}=

\displaystyle\frac{\,2^{\frac{3}{4}}}{\sqrt{G_{F}}\,\Lambda^{2}}\left(C_{\varphi G}+iC_{\varphi\tilde{G}}\right)

(116)

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{\frac{1}{4}}}{G_{F}^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\Lambda^{4}}\left(C_{\varphi G}+iC_{\varphi\tilde{G}}\right)\left(C_{\varphi\Box}-\frac{1}{4}C_{\varphi D}+2C_{\varphi G}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{\frac{1}{4}}}{G_{F}^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\Lambda^{4}}\left(C_{G^{2}\varphi^{4}}^{(1)}+iC_{G^{2}\varphi^{4}}^{(2)}\right),

HEFT:

\displaystyle-i\,c_{ggh}=

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{S}^{2}}{2^{3/4}}\sqrt{G_{F}}\left(C_{GH}^{(1)}+i\,C_{\tilde{G}H}^{(1)}\right).

(117)

E.1.2
hh\to h

For the coefficient of the 3-Higgs boson on-shell amplitude, the matching with SMEFT yields,

\displaystyle-i\,c_{3h}=

\displaystyle 3\sqrt[4]{2}\sqrt{G_{F}}m_{h}^{2}+\frac{3}{8\,G_{F}^{3/2}\,\Lambda^{2}}\left(-8\sqrt[4]{2}C^{\varphi}+12\ 2^{3/4}\,C_{\varphi\Box}\,G_{F}\ m_{h}^{2}-3\ 2^{3/4}C_{\varphi D}G_{F}\ m_{h}^{2}\right)

(118)

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{64\,G_{F}^{5/2}\Lambda^{4}}\left(96\ 2^{3/4}C_{\varphi}C_{\varphi\Box}-24\ 2^{3/4}C_{\varphi}C_{\varphi D}-192\sqrt[4]{2}C_{\varphi^{6}\Box}\,G_{F}\,m_{h}^{2}+64\ 2^{3/4}C_{\varphi^{8}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+20\sqrt[4]{2}C_{\varphi^{6}D^{2}}G_{F}m_{h}^{2}-240\sqrt[4]{2}(C_{\varphi\Box})^{2}G_{F}m_{h}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+120\sqrt[4]{2}C_{\varphi\Box}C_{\varphi D}G_{F}m_{h}^{2}-15\sqrt[4]{2}(C_{\varphi D})^{2}G_{F}m_{h}^{2}\right).

In HEFT, we have,

\displaystyle-i\,c_{3h}=

\displaystyle 3\,m_{h}^{2}\sqrt[4]{2}\sqrt{G_{F}}\left(1+\Delta\lambda_{3}\right).

(119)

E.2
Four-point amplitudes

E.3
hh\to hh

Here, we expand the on-shell coefficient as,

c_{4h}=c_{4h}^{(00)}+\frac{c_{4h}^{(10)}}{\Lambda^{2}}\,s_{12}+\frac{c_{4h}^{(01)}}{\Lambda^{2}}\,s_{13}+\frac{c_{4h}^{(20)}}{\Lambda^{4}}\,s_{12}^{2}+\frac{c_{4h}^{(02)}}{\Lambda^{4}}\,s_{13}^{2}+\frac{c_{4h}^{(11)}}{\Lambda^{4}}\,s_{12}\,s_{13}+...\,,

(120)

which can be matched to SMEFT up to dimension 8 in the
G_{F}
input scheme to yield,

\displaystyle-i\,c_{4h}^{(00)}=

\displaystyle 3\ m_{h}^{2}\sqrt{2}\ G_{F}+\frac{2}{\Lambda^{2}}\left(-\frac{9\sqrt{2}C_{\varphi}}{G_{F}}+25C_{\varphi\Box}m_{h}^{2}-\frac{25C_{\varphi D}m_{h}^{2}}{4}\right)

(121)

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{G_{F}^{2}\ \Lambda^{4}}\left(36C_{\varphi}C_{\varphi\Box}-9C_{\varphi}C_{\varphi D}+16C_{\varphi^{8}}\right)-6\frac{m_{h}^{4}}{\Lambda^{4}}\left(C_{\varphi^{4}D^{4}}^{(1)}+C_{\varphi^{4}D^{4}}^{(2)}+C_{\varphi^{4}D^{4}}^{(3)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{4\sqrt{2}}\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{G_{F}\ \Lambda^{4}}\left(172C_{\varphi^{6}\Box}-21C_{\varphi^{6}D^{2}}+22(C_{\varphi D}-4C_{\varphi\Box})^{2}\right),

\displaystyle-i\,c_{4h}^{(10)}=

\displaystyle\frac{4m_{h}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\left(C_{\varphi^{4}D^{4}}^{(1)}+C_{\varphi^{4}D^{4}}^{(2)}+C_{\varphi^{4}D^{4}}^{(3)}\right),

\displaystyle c_{4h}^{(01)}=

\displaystyle c_{4h}^{(10)}\,,

\displaystyle-i\,c_{4h}^{(20)}=

\displaystyle-\left(C_{\varphi^{4}D^{4}}^{(1)}+C_{\varphi^{4}D^{4}}^{(2)}+C_{\varphi^{4}D^{4}}^{(3)}\right),

\displaystyle c_{4h}^{(02)}=

\displaystyle c_{4h}^{(20)}\,,

\displaystyle c_{4h}^{(11)}=

\displaystyle c_{4h}^{(20)}\,.

In HEFT up to NNLO, we find

\displaystyle-i\,c_{4h}^{(00)}=

\displaystyle 3\ m_{h}^{2}\sqrt{2}\ G_{F}\left(1+\Delta\lambda_{4}\right)-16\ m_{h}^{2}G_{F}^{2}(C_{DH}^{(1)}\,{C^{\prime}}_{DH}^{(1)})^{2},

(122)

\displaystyle-i\,c_{4h}^{(10)}=

\displaystyle 32\,m_{h}^{2}G_{F}^{2}\overline{\Lambda}^{2}(C_{DH}^{(1)}\,{C^{\prime}}_{DH}^{(1)})^{2},

\displaystyle-i\ c_{4h}^{(01)}=
